新闻简报ReliefWeb 于 2026 年 11 月 17 日发布的这份报告概述了南苏丹为 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 所做的准备工作。报告指出,南苏丹尚未报告任何埃博拉病毒病确诊病例,但强调了由于刚果民主共和国(刚果民主共和国)持续发生的 传播 疫情带来的输入风险。截至 2026 年 11 月 17 日,该国已有 10,920 例确诊病例,5,323 例死亡。靠近南苏丹边境的栋古报告了一例确诊病例,引发担忧。报告强调,南苏丹已被世界卫生组织(WHO)确定为非洲埃博拉病毒病输入风险最高的国家。作为回应,南苏丹于 2026 年 10 月 19 日至 23 日进行了全国全面模拟演习 (FSX),在朱巴、耶伊和延比奥三个中心的 10 个地点模拟了埃博拉病毒病检测场景。此次演习测试了九个准备和响应支柱,并确定了响应链中的关键弱点,需要有针对性的纠正行动和额外支持。报告还提到,已经对可信警报进行了调查,这些警报主要来自靠近刚果民主共和国边境和首都朱巴的延比奥和马格维等高风险县。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于2026年11月17日发布。全国全面模拟演习(FSX)于2026年10月19日至23日进行。刚果民主共和国暴发数据指的是截至2026年11月17日的持续情况。
地点与对象
南苏丹,特别关注首都朱巴以及与刚果民主共和国(DRC)接壤的边境地区,包括上韦莱省以及高风险县延比奥和马圭。
事件
继之前的桌面模拟和演习之后,南苏丹进行了一次全国全面模拟演习(FSX),以测试其对 埃博拉病毒病(EVD)的准备情况。该报告还强调了由于刚果民主共和国持续发生的 传播 和跨境流动而带来的埃博拉病毒病进口风险。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 11 月 17 日,刚果民主共和国 暴发 已有 10,920 例确诊病例,5,323 例死亡。靠近南苏丹边境的栋古报告了一例确诊病例。世界卫生组织确定南苏丹是非洲埃博拉病毒病输入风险最高的国家。
应对、状态与不确定性
南苏丹进行了一次全国全面模拟演习(FSX),以评估其埃博拉病毒病的准备情况。此次演习发现了响应链中的关键弱点,需要有针对性的纠正行动和额外支持。该报告是一份准备建议,而不是确认的 暴发。
A high priorityPublic-health news

South Sudan: Ebola virus disease preparedness: Costed interim readiness proposal - ReliefWeb

News Brief
This report from ReliefWeb, published on 17 November 2026, outlines South Sudan's preparedness efforts for Ebola virus disease (EVD). It notes that South Sudan has not reported any confirmed EVD cases but highlights the risk of importation due to ongoing transmission in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), which has 10,920 confirmed cases and 5,323 deaths as of 17 November 2026. A confirmed case was reported in Dungu, near the South Sudan border, raising concerns. The report emphasizes that South Sudan has been identified by the World Health Organization (WHO) as having the highest risk of EVD importation in Africa. In response, South Sudan conducted a national full-scale simulation exercise (FSX) from 19 to 23 October 2026, simulating EVD detection scenarios at 10 sites across three hubs: Juba, Yei, and Yambio. The exercise tested nine preparedness and response pillars and identified critical weaknesses in the response chain, requiring targeted corrective actions and additional support. The report also mentions that credible alerts have been investigated, primarily from high-risk counties like Yambio and Magwi, near the DRC border and the capital Juba.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Help Log in ReliefWeb Search Content Search What are you looking for? Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA South Sudan South Sudan Ebola virus disease preparedness: Costed interim readiness proposal Format Manual and Guideline Source OCHA Posted 17 Nov 2026 Originally published 17 Nov 2026 Attachments Download Report (PDF | 1.04 MB) Introduction South Sudan has not reported any confirmed Ebola virus disease (EVD) cases. However, ongoing transmission in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), coupled with continued cross-border movements through formal and informal entry points, poses a high risk of importation. The DRC outbreak continues to expand, with 10,920 confirmed cases and 5,323 deaths, representing a case fatality rate of 48.7 per cent across 67 health zones in 8 provinces. Haut-Uele Province, which borders Western and Central Equatoria, has reported 486 cases and 213 deaths, including a confirmed case in Dungu, near the South Sudan border – a development of particular concern. The recent importation of cases into Kenya and Nigeria further underscores the risk of cross-border transmission, with the World Health Organization (WHO) identifying South Sudan as having the highest risk of EVD importation in Africa. As part of national preparedness efforts, South Sudan conducted a national full-scale simulation exercise (FSX) from 19 to 23 October 2026. The exercise was nationally coordinated and implemented with technical support from the WHO, the United Nations Children’s Fund, the Africa Centre for Disease Control and Prevention and the FHI 360 STRIDES project. It followed the tabletop simulation exercise completed in June 2026, as well as multiple drills conducted in laboratories, newly established isolation facilities and several training sites. The FSX simulated EVD detection scenarios at 10 sites across 3 hubs: Juba (4 sites), Yei (3 sites) and Yambio (3 sites). The scenarios were designed to test nine EVD preparedness and response pillars. South Sudan has also investigated multiple credible alerts largely originating from the 15 high-risk counties, with the highest number of alerts coming from Yambio and Magwi, near the border with DRC and Juba, the capital. These alerts complement the latest WHO risk estimates, which have identified the cities of Rumbek, Bor and Wau as additional geographical areas to consider when strengthening EVD preparedness. Following six months of EVD preparedness and readiness operations, the FSX documented strengths in early detection. However, the exercise also identified a number of critical weaknesses at key operational handover points across the response chain, requiring targeted corrective action and additional support. Event
South Sudan conducted a national full-scale simulation exercise (FSX) to test its preparedness for Ebola virus disease (EVD), following previous tabletop simulations and drills. The report also highlights the risk of EVD importation due to ongoing transmission in the DRC and cross-border movements.
Scale, impact and risk
The DRC outbreak has 10,920 confirmed cases and 5,323 deaths as of 17 November 2026. A confirmed case was reported in Dungu, near the South Sudan border. The WHO identified South Sudan as having the highest risk of EVD importation in Africa.
Response, status and uncertainty
South Sudan has conducted a national full-scale simulation exercise (FSX) to evaluate its EVD preparedness. The exercise identified critical weaknesses in the response chain, requiring targeted corrective actions and additional support. The report is a preparedness proposal, not a confirmed outbreak.

多国 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 情况报告第 26 号(2026 年 11 月 2 – 8 日) - ReliefWeb

Multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak Situation Report No. 26 (2 – 8 November 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 2 日至 8 日发布的第 26 号多国 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 情况报告强调了刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 正在进行的 暴发,其中 传播 集中在伊图里和北基伍地区。截至 11 月 7 日,67 个卫生区和 271 个 PoE/PoC 地点受到影响。肯尼亚无新增确诊病例,报告期内已有5名患者出院。在 IOM 支持的 PoE/PoC 中拦截了 7 名死者,使累计总数达到 266 人。死者被转交给 监测 团队进行样本收集,并转交给安全和有尊严的埋葬 (SDB) 团队进行埋葬。此外,还发现了 35 个警报,其中 20 个已得到验证/转介。该报告于2026年11月13日发布,说明了暴发的当前状态以及正在采取的应对措施。

Multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak Situation Report No. 26 (2 – 8 November 2026) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
The Multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak Situation Report No. 26, dated 2–8 November 2026, highlights the ongoing outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), with transmission concentrated in Ituri and North Kivu regions. As of 7 November, 67 health zones and 271 PoE/PoC locations were affected. In Kenya, no new confirmed cases were reported, and five patients were discharged during the reporting period. Seven deceased persons were intercepted at IOM-supported PoEs/PoCs, bringing the cumulative total to 266. The deceased were referred to the surveillance team for sample collection and to the Safe and Dignified Burial (SDB) team for burial. Additionally, 35 alerts were identified, of which 20 were verified/referred. The report was posted on 13 November 2026, indicating the current status of the outbreak and the response measures being taken.

世卫组织总干事在关于刚果民主共和国本迪布焦病毒病疫情的《国际卫生条例》突发事件委员会第三次会议上的开幕致辞 – 11 月 17 日

WHO Director-General's opening remarks at the third IHR Emergency Committee meeting on Bundibugyo virus disease epidemic in the Democratic Republic of the Congo – 17 November ...
新闻简报世卫组织总干事于 2026 年 11 月 17 日在《国际卫生条例》突发事件委员会第三次会议上就刚果民主共和国本迪布焦病毒病疫情发表讲话。暴发 已持续六个月,自上次委员会会议以来,病例数增加了一倍多。截至目前,八个省的67个卫生区已报告超过11,000例病例和5,400例死亡。五岁以下儿童尤其容易受到伤害,超过四分之一的确诊病例和三分之一的死亡病例。肯尼亚报告了 9 例与输入病例有关的病例,其中 2 例死亡,而尼日利亚报告了 1 例输入病例。尽管在某些领域取得了进展,但 暴发 仍在扩大,不安全因素构成了重大挑战。Cantine-Aloya 的袭击造成 66 名平民死亡,其中包括三名卫生工作者,并扰乱了行动。接触者追踪仍低于目标,三分之二的死亡发生在 治疗 中心之外,这表明感染识别较晚。研究正在取得进展,针对本迪布焦病毒的治疗临床试验和 疫苗 试验,尽管 疫苗 并不能替代早期护理和安全埋葬等即时措施。由于一线工人工资延迟和持续的安全威胁,应对措施受到阻碍。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年11月17日(活动日期),2026年11月16日(发布日期)。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国,八个省的 67 个卫生区报告了病例。五岁以下儿童受到的影响尤为严重,超过四分之一的确诊病例和三分之一的死亡病例。
事件
世卫组织总干事在关于刚果民主共和国本迪布焦病毒病疫情的《国际卫生条例》突发事件委员会第三次会议上致开幕词。暴发 已经持续了六个月,自委员会上次会议以来,案件数量增加了一倍多。
规模、影响与风险
已报告超过 11,000 例病例和 5,400 例死亡。每周仍报告 400 至 500 例新病例,并且 暴发 仍在某些地区扩大。肯尼亚报告了九例与输入病例有关的病例,其中两人死亡。尼日利亚报告一例输入病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
肯尼亚和尼日利亚对病例的快速发现和管理显示了准备、透明度和迅速控制措施的价值。然而,暴发仍在一些地区扩张,不安全是一个主要障碍。三分之二的确诊死亡病例仍然发生在埃博拉 治疗 中心之外,这表明感染识别较晚。联系后续行动仍低于目标。研究正在取得进展,针对本迪布焦病毒的治疗临床试验和 疫苗 试验。
A high priorityPublic-health news

WHO Director-General's opening remarks at the third IHR Emergency Committee meeting on Bundibugyo virus disease epidemic in the Democratic Republic of the Congo – 17 November ...

News Brief
The WHO Director-General addressed the third IHR Emergency Committee meeting on the Bundibugyo virus disease epidemic in the Democratic Republic of the Congo on 17 November 2026. The outbreak, which has been ongoing for six months, has seen the number of cases more than double since the last committee meeting. As of now, over 11,000 cases and 5,400 deaths have been reported across 67 health zones in eight provinces. Children under five are particularly vulnerable, with more than a quarter of confirmed cases and one third of deaths. Kenya has reported nine cases linked to an imported case, including two deaths, while Nigeria has reported one imported case. Despite progress in some areas, the outbreak is still expanding, and insecurity poses a significant challenge. An attack on Cantine-Aloya killed 66 civilians, including three health workers, and disrupted operations. Contact tracing remains below target, and two-thirds of deaths occur outside treatment centres, indicating late identification of infections. Research is advancing, with clinical trials of therapeutics and vaccine trials for Bundibugyo virus, though vaccines are not a substitute for immediate measures like early care and safe burials. The response is hindered by delays in paying frontline workers and ongoing security threats.
Original source text (English)

Member States Portal Home / Newsroom / Speeches / item / WHO Director-General's opening remarks at the third IHR Emergency Committee meeting on Bundibugyo virus disease epidemic in the Democratic Republic of the Congo – 17 November 2026 WHO Director-General's opening remarks at the third IHR Emergency Committee meeting on Bundibugyo virus disease epidemic in the Democratic Republic of the Congo – 17 November 2026 17 November 2026 Remarks Geneva Reading time: Chair, Professor Lucille Blumberg, Members of the Emergency Committee, dear colleagues and friends, Thank you once again for your commitment to this Committee. I also thank the representatives of the Central African Republic, the Democratic Republic of the Congo, Kenya, Nigeria, South Sudan and Uganda for joining us today. Six months have now passed since this outbreak was declared, and three months since this Committee last met. In that time, the number of cases has more than doubled. More than 11 000 cases and 5400 deaths have now been reported, in 67 health zones across eight provinces. Children are paying a terrible price. They account for more than a quarter of confirmed cases and one third of deaths. Seven in ten children under five with confirmed Ebola have died. The risk beyond the DRC’s borders is not theoretical. Kenya has reported nine cases linked to an imported case, including two deaths. Its last patient has been discharged, and the 42-day countdown has begun. Nigeria has reported one imported case. In both countries, rapid detection and management of cases show the value of preparedness, transparency and swift control measures. I remain concerned that some countries continue to apply blanket travel restrictions. They hinder the response, damage livelihoods and discourage countries from reporting outbreaks quickly. Targeted, evidence-based measures work better. There is also real progress. Rapid transmission has been halted in most of the early epicentres. At the start of the outbreak, two laboratories could test for the virus. Today there are more than two dozen. Close to 2000 treatment beds are now available, and about 3000 people have recovered. And research is progressing. Clinical trials of therapeutics are progressing, with more than 900 people enrolled; Last month, a vaccine trial began using the Ervebo vaccine, which is approved for Ebola virus, but not for Bundibugyo virus. Two candidate vaccines developed specifically for Bundibugyo virus will be added to the trial in the coming weeks. But vaccines are not a substitute for the measures that save lives now: early care, contact tracing, isolation, safe and dignified burials, and trusted engagement with communities. But progress in some places is not control of the outbreak as a whole. Between 400 and 500 new cases are still reported every week, and the outbreak is still expanding in some areas. When this Committee last met, I said that what concerned me most was where people are dying. That has not changed. Two-thirds of confirmed deaths are still occurring outside of Ebola treatment centres, which tells us that infections are being identified too late. Contact follow-up remains below target. Insecurity is now one of the greatest obstacles to the response. Earlier this month, an attack on the town of Cantine-Aloya killed 66 civilians, including three health workers. Health facilities were looted and burned, and more than 1000 contacts were lost to follow-up. Two weeks ago a nurse was killed and another health worker badly injured when they were attacked while responding to an Ebola alert in Tshopo province. These incidents restrict humanitarian access, disrupt operations, make it harder to reach communities quickly, and give the virus an advantage – the virus can freely move while the response is restricted. Delays in paying frontline health workers and responders are also causing tension and slowing the response. And we must always remember that Ebola is just one of the threats these communities face, which means the response must also protect essential health and humanitarian services. Outbreak control cannot come at the expense of primary health care, maternal and child health services, or access to treatment for other diseases. Once again, thank you all for your time and expertise. I look forward to your assessment of the situation, and to your advice. I thank you. Related IHR Emergency Committee regarding the epidemic of Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease Ebola outbreak – Democratic Republic of the Congo 2026 WHO's work on Ebola disease

WHO Director-General made opening remarks at the third IHR Emergency Committee meeting on the Bundibugyo virus disease epidemic in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. The outbreak has been ongoing for six months, with the number of cases more than doubling since the last meeting of the committee.
Scale, impact and risk

Response, status and uncertainty

However, the outbreak is still expanding in some areas, and insecurity is a major obstacle. Two-thirds of confirmed deaths are still occurring outside of Ebola treatment centres, indicating late identification of infections. Contact follow-up remains below target. Research is progressing, with clinical trials of therapeutics and vaccine trials for Bundibugyo virus.

News Brief
The WHO Regional Office for Africa released a news update on 12 November 2026, highlighting its efforts to assist South Sudan in preparing for the potential outbreak of Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease. The text emphasizes the importance of readiness and response strategies, but it does not report any confirmed cases, deaths, or active spread of the virus. The preparation activities are part of broader health emergency response initiatives, with no specific details on the scale of the threat or the number of people affected. The event is described as a preventive measure, indicating that the outbreak has not yet occurred. The response status is under investigation and preparation, with no further details provided on the current situation or risk level.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

人道主义协调员呼吁加强南苏丹的埃博拉准备工作 - ReliefWeb

Humanitarian Coordinator calls for strengthened Ebola readiness in South Sudan - ReliefWeb
新闻简报In November 2026, the United Nations Resident Coordinator and Humanitarian Coordinator in South Sudan, Mr. Ramanathan Balakrishnan, called for increased preparedness to respond to a potential Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak. This call followed a full-scale simulation exercise conducted in October 2026 by the Ministry of Health, the National Public Health Institute (NPHI), the World Health Organization (WHO), and partners. The exercise assessed South Sudan's capacity to detect, notify, investigate, and respond to an EVD outbreak, identifying significant gaps in alert management, rapid response team management, health worker remuneration, ambulance services, laboratory facilities, personal protective equipment supplies, contact tracing, and screening arrangements at points of entry. To address these challenges, a three-month plan was launched to mobilise USD$7 million in financial resources for strengthening preparedness and response capacities. The Humanitarian Coordinator urged donors and partners to support this initiative, emphasizing the importance of national government leadership and ensuring the safety and security of humanitarian workers.
查看新闻五要素
时间
联合国南苏丹驻地协调员兼人道主义协调员拉马纳坦·巴拉克里希南先生呼吁2026年11月加强准备。模拟演习于2026年10月进行。
地点与对象
南苏丹,重点关注该国的卫生系统及其人口。
事件
联合国驻南苏丹驻地协调员和人道主义协调员呼吁加强准备和准备,以应对潜在的 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 暴发。这一呼吁是在卫生部、国家公共卫生研究所 (NPHI)、世界卫生组织 (WHO) 和合作伙伴进行模拟演习后做出的。
规模、影响与风险
模拟演习突显了警报管理、快速反应团队管理、卫生工作者薪酬、救护车服务、实验室设施、个人防护设备供应、接触者追踪和入境点筛查安排方面的重大差距。这些差距对国家检测、遏制和应对潜在埃博拉病毒病 暴发 的能力构成风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
The Humanitarian Coordinator has launched a three-month prioritised plan to mobilise financial resources to address the operational and capacity gaps identified during the simulation exercise. The plan requests USD$7 million to strengthen preparedness and response capacities.
A high priorityPublic-health news

Humanitarian Coordinator calls for strengthened Ebola readiness in South Sudan - ReliefWeb

News Brief
In November 2026, the United Nations Resident Coordinator and Humanitarian Coordinator in South Sudan, Mr. Ramanathan Balakrishnan, called for increased preparedness to respond to a potential Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak. This call followed a full-scale simulation exercise conducted in October 2026 by the Ministry of Health, the National Public Health Institute (NPHI), the World Health Organization (WHO), and partners. The exercise assessed South Sudan's capacity to detect, notify, investigate, and respond to an EVD outbreak, identifying significant gaps in alert management, rapid response team management, health worker remuneration, ambulance services, laboratory facilities, personal protective equipment supplies, contact tracing, and screening arrangements at points of entry. To address these challenges, a three-month plan was launched to mobilise USD$7 million in financial resources for strengthening preparedness and response capacities. The Humanitarian Coordinator urged donors and partners to support this initiative, emphasizing the importance of national government leadership and ensuring the safety and security of humanitarian workers.
Original source text (English)

Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA South Sudan Humanitarian Coordinator calls for strengthened Ebola readiness in South Sudan Format News and Press Release Source OCHA Posted 17 Nov 2026 Originally published 17 Nov 2026 Attachments Download Report (PDF | 168.32 KB) Juba, 17 November 2026 – The United Nations Resident Coordinator and Humanitarian Coordinator in South Sudan, Mr. Ramanathan Balakrishnan, has called for increased preparedness and readiness to respond to a potential Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak in the country. The call follows a recent full-scale simulation exercise conducted in October 2026 by the Ministry of Health, the National Public Health Institute (NPHI), the World Health Organization (WHO) and partners to assess the country's capacity to detect, notify, investigate and respond to a potential EVD outbreak. The exercise assessed key areas of South Sudan’s Ebola preparedness efforts, including case management, infection prevention and control, laboratory capacity, contact tracing and coordination. While the exercise demonstrated the system’s capacity to quickly detect simulated cases, significant gaps remain in alert management, rapid response team management, health worker remuneration, ambulance services, laboratory facilities, personal protective equipment supplies, contact tracing, and screening arrangements at points of entry. To address these challenges, the Humanitarian Coordinator has launched a three-month prioritised plan to mobilise financial resources to address the operational and capacity gaps identified during the simulation exercise. He called on donors and partners to support the plan, which requests USD$7 million to strengthen preparedness and response capacities. “While there are no reported cases of Ebola in South Sudan, closing these preparedness gaps is critical to ensure that South Sudan is better prepared to detect, contain and respond to a potential outbreak, and to protect the lives and well-being of its people,” Mr. Balakrishnan said. Reiterating the importance of national government leadership in strengthening EVD preparedness, Mr. Balakrishnan said, “The payment of health worker salaries by the government authorities is critical, as is facilitating access for Ebola-related movements for both health actors and UNMISS and ensuring the safety and security of humanitarian workers.” Disclaimer UN Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs To learn more about OCHA's activities, please visit https://www.unocha.org/. The United Nations Resident Coordinator and Humanitarian Coordinator in South Sudan called for increased preparedness and readiness to respond to a potential Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak. This call was made following a simulation exercise conducted by the Ministry of Health, the National Public Health Institute (NPHI), the World Health Organization (WHO), and partners.
Scale, impact and risk
The simulation exercise highlighted significant gaps in alert management, rapid response team management, health worker remuneration, ambulance services, laboratory facilities, personal protective equipment supplies, contact tracing, and screening arrangements at points of entry. These gaps pose a risk to the country's ability to detect, contain, and respond to a potential EVD outbreak.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Humanitarian Coordinator has launched a three-month prioritised plan to mobilise financial resources to address the operational and capacity gaps identified during the simulation exercise. The plan requests USD$7 million to strengthen preparedness and response capacities.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

吉利德通过自愿许可扩大了治疗埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病的研究型奥贝昔韦的使用范围

Gilead Expands Access to Investigational Obeldesivir for Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Through Voluntary Licensing
新闻简报吉利德科学公司通过与三个仿制药制造商签订免版税自愿许可协议,扩大了其在研 抗病毒药物 奥贝昔韦的使用范围,未来可能用作埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 的暴露后预防 (PEP)。这些协议主要覆盖 127 个低收入和中低收入国家,包括所有报告 BVD 病例的非洲国家。该计划是吉利德为 暴发 场景做准备、支持制造准备、技术转让和供应规划的更广泛战略的一部分。由 ALIMA 和合作伙伴协调进行的 EBO-PEP 现场试验正在评估奥贝昔韦作为 PEP 对 BVD 高风险个体的疗效。然而,奥贝昔韦目前尚未在任何国家被批准用于丝状病毒病的 PEP,其对 BVD 的临床疗效仍未得到证实。吉利德还向肯尼亚、刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达捐赠了瑞德西韦,用于治疗 BVD 确诊病例的研究用途。这些协议反映了吉利德的承诺,即确保有效的药物到达有需要的人手中,等待监管部门的批准和临床数据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年11月15日。事件日期:提供的证据中未报告。时间范围:正在进行的现场试验和未来可能的访问。
地点与对象
国家:127 个主要是低收入和中低收入国家,包括在当前埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 暴发 期间报告病例的所有非洲国家。受影响人群:感染埃博拉 BVD 的高风险人群,包括密切接触者。
事件
吉利德科学公司宣布与三个仿制药制造商签署免版税自愿许可协议,以支持未来可能使用其在研 抗病毒药物 奥贝昔韦作为埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 的暴露后预防 (PEP)。这些协议是正在进行的临床研究的一部分,旨在支持生产准备、技术转让和供应规划。
规模、影响与风险
规模:127 个国家,包括所有报告病例的非洲国家。影响:未来可能会获得奥贝昔韦用于 PEP。风险:未建立;奥贝昔韦在任何国家均未获批准用于丝状病毒病的 PEP。埃博拉 BVD 的临床疗效尚未得到证实。
应对、状态与不确定性
调查:正在进行的现场试验(EBO-PEP)评估奥贝昔韦作为 BVD 的 PEP。不确定性:奥贝昔韦需要临床数据和监管部门批准才能获得授权使用。尚未确认结果。吉利德还向肯尼亚、刚果民主共和国和乌干达捐赠了瑞德西韦用于研究用途。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Gilead Expands Access to Investigational Obeldesivir for Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Through Voluntary Licensing

News Brief
Gilead Sciences has expanded access to its investigational antiviral obeldesivir for potential future use as post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) for Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD) through royalty-free voluntary licensing agreements with three generic manufacturers. These agreements cover 127 primarily low- and lower-middle-income countries, including all African nations reporting BVD cases. The initiative is part of Gilead’s broader strategy to prepare for outbreak scenarios, supporting manufacturing readiness, technology transfer, and supply planning. The ongoing EBO-PEP field trial, coordinated by ALIMA and partners, is evaluating obeldesivir’s efficacy as PEP for individuals at high risk of developing BVD. However, obeldesivir is not currently approved for PEP of filovirus disease in any country, and its clinical efficacy for BVD remains unconfirmed. Gilead has also donated remdesivir to Kenya, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), and Uganda for investigational use in treating confirmed BVD cases. The agreements reflect Gilead’s commitment to ensuring effective medicines reach those in need, pending regulatory approvals and clinical data.
Original source text (English)
FOSTER CITY, Calif.--(BUSINESS WIRE)--Nov 16, 2026-- Gilead Sciences, Inc. (Nasdaq: GILD) today announced that it has signed royalty-free voluntary licensing agreements with three generic manufacturers to support potential future access to its investigational antiviral obeldesivir as post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) for Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD). The agreements cover 127 primarily low- and lower-middle-income countries, consistent with the geographic scope of Gilead’s existing voluntary licensing agreements for remdesivir for COVID-19 and include all African countries that have reported cases during the current Ebola BVD outbreak. The agreements are intended to support manufacturing readiness, technology transfer and supply planning while the ongoing Ebola Post-Exposure Prophylaxis (EBO-PEP) field trial evaluates obeldesivir as PEP for Ebola BVD. Key Highlights Royalty-free voluntary licensing agreements with three generic manufacturers cover primarily low- and lower-middle-income countries and are intended to support potential future access to investigational obeldesivir. EBO-PEP is a Phase 3 randomized, placebo-controlled trial being coordinated by ALIMA and partners evaluating investigational obeldesivir as PEP for individuals at high risk of developing Ebola BVD. Gilead is updating its existing voluntary licensing agreements for remdesivir to include filovirus disease, supporting potential future access should results from the ongoing World Health Organization (WHO)-sponsored PARTNERS trial support its use as a treatment for Ebola BVD and pending potential regulatory approvals. Gilead recently donated more than 2,000 vials of remdesivir to Kenya for investigational use as treatment and PEP for people with confirmed Ebola BVD or individuals with a close contact exposure. “Responding to emerging infectious diseases, including Ebola virus disease, requires not only scientific innovation, but also early planning and collaboration to help ensure that effective medicines can reach the people who need them as quickly as possible,” said Jaime McCoy, Vice President, Gilead Patient Solutions, Gilead Sciences. “These agreements reflect our commitment to preparing for future access while clinical research is ongoing, so that we are better positioned to respond if these investigational medicines demonstrate benefit and gain regulatory approval.” The manufacturers that have entered into voluntary licensing agreements with Gilead are Dr. Reddy’s Laboratories, Eva Pharma and Ferozsons Laboratories. Technology transfer discussions are already underway, and agreements with additional manufacturers are under discussion. Under the agreements, the companies will be authorized to manufacture and supply obeldesivir in the licensed territory if supported by clinical data and applicable regulatory authorizations. Obeldesivir has demonstrated preclinical activity across multiple filoviruses but is not approved for the PEP of filovirus disease in any country. These agreements are part of Gilead's broader approach to outbreak preparedness, which includes clinical research, manufacturing and supply planning, technology transfer, and collaboration with governments, public health agencies and global health partners. As part of its response to the current Ebola BVD outbreak, Gilead has provided compassionate use donations of remdesivir to the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), Uganda and Kenya totaling more than 38,000 vials. About Obeldesivir Obeldesivir is an investigational antiviral that has been evaluated in clinical trials for COVID-19 and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and has demonstrated preclinical activity across multiple filoviruses. It is currently being evaluated in the Ebola Post-Exposure Prophylaxis (EBO-PEP) trial, a partner-led Phase 3 randomized, placebo-controlled field trial evaluating the efficacy of obeldesivir as post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) for individuals at high risk of developing Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD). Obeldesivir is not approved for the PEP of filovirus disease or for any indication in any country. About Remdesivir Remdesivir is a nucleotide analog prodrug invented and developed by Gilead, building on more than a decade of the company’s antiviral research. Remdesivir has broad-spectrum antiviral activity both in vitro and in animal models against multiple viral pathogens, including Marburg, Ebola, SARS, MERS and SARS-CoV-2, the virus that causes COVID-19. Remdesivir has been approved as a COVID-19 treatment in approximately 50 countries worldwide. To date, remdesivir has been made available to more than 14.5 million patients around the world, including more than 8.1 million people in middle- and low-income countries through Gilead’s voluntary licensing program. The clinical efficacy of remdesivir for the treatment of Ebola BVD has not been established. Remdesivir is currently not approved in any country for the treatment or prevention of Ebola BVD. For more information about Gilead’s commitment to global health, visit Gilead.com . About Gilead Sciences Gilead Sciences, Inc. is a biopharmaceutical company that has pursued and achieved breakthroughs in medicine for more than three decades, with the goal of creating a healthier world for all people. The company is committed to advancing innovative medicines to prevent and treat life-threatening diseases, including HIV, viral hepatitis, COVID-19, cancer and inflammation. In 2025, Gilead announced a $32 billion investment to further strengthen its U.S. footprint to power the next era of discovery, job creation and public health preparedness – while continuing to invest globally to ensure patients everywhere benefit from its scientific innovation. Gilead operates in more than 35 countries worldwide, with headquarters in Foster City, Calif. Forward-Looking Statements This press release includes forward-looking statements within the meaning of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995 that are subject to risks, uncertainties and other factors, including Gilead’s ability to initiate, progress or complete clinical trials within currently anticipated timelines or at all, and the possibility of unfavorable results from ongoing or additional clinical trials, including those involving obeldesivir and remdesivir (such as the partner-led EBO-PEP and PARTNERS trials); uncertainties relating to regulatory applications and related filing and approval timelines, including additional pending and potential applications for obeldesivir and remdesivir; the risk that any regulatory approvals, if granted, may be subject to significant limitations on use or subject to withdrawal or other adverse actions by the applicable regulatory authority; the possibility that Gilead may make a strategic decision to discontinue development of obeldesivir and remdesivir for indications currently under evaluation; Gilead’s ability to effectively manage, execute and realize the anticipated benefits from the access strategy relating to obeldesivir and remdesivir, including voluntary licensing arrangements and external partnerships relating to obeldesivir and remdesivir; difficulties or unanticipated expenses in connection with such access strategy, licensing arrangements and partnerships; and any assumptions underlying any of the foregoing. These and other risks, uncertainties and factors are described in detail in Gilead’s Quarterly Report on Form 10-Q for the quarter ended September 30, 2026, as filed with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission. The agreements are part of ongoing clinical research and are intended to support manufacturing readiness, technology transfer, and supply planning.
Scale, impact and risk
Scale: 127 countries, including all African countries with reported cases. Impact: Potential future access to obeldesivir for PEP. Risk: Not established; obeldesivir is not approved for PEP of filovirus disease in any country. Clinical efficacy for Ebola BVD is not yet confirmed.
Response, status and uncertainty
Investigation: Ongoing field trial (EBO-PEP) evaluating obeldesivir as PEP for BVD. Uncertainty: Clinical data and regulatory approvals are required for obeldesivir to be authorized for use. No confirmed results yet. Gilead has also donated remdesivir to Kenya, DRC, and Uganda for investigational use.
原文 / Source
Published: 2026-11-15  |  Publisher: CGTN

谭德塞:“六个月前,我宣布刚果民主共和国的本迪布焦病毒埃博拉暴发为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。自那时以来,八个省份已记录了超过 11,000 例病例和 5400 例死亡,使其成为规模最大的省份 - CGTN

Tedros: 'Six months ago, I declared the Bundibugyo virus Ebola outbreak in the DRC a Public Health Emergency of International Concern. Since then, over 11,000 cases and 5400 deaths have been recorded across eight provinces, making this the largest and - CGTN
新闻简报世卫组织总干事谭德塞于 2026 年 5 月 17 日宣布刚果民主共和国(DRC)的本迪布焦病毒埃博拉病毒 暴发 为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(PHEIC)。截至 2026 年 11 月 15 日,暴发 已在八个省份造成超过 11,000 例病例和 5400 人死亡,使其成为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。刚果民主共和国历史上规模最大、最致命的埃博拉病毒 暴发。儿童受到的影响尤为严重。尽管面临挑战,当地应对人员和全球合作伙伴仍在取得进展,传播 在几个早期震中的进展放缓,临床试验进展迅速。刚果民主共和国的总体风险仍然很高,邻国也很高,但由于做好准备和快速检测措施,区域和全球层面的风险较低。谭德塞对刚果医护人员的努力表示感谢,并强调在应对工作中继续合作的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 15 日(发布日期)、2026 年 5 月 17 日(活动日期)。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的八个省份,重点是北基伍省和贝尼卫生区。
事件
六个月前,世卫组织总干事谭德塞宣布刚果民主共和国爆发的本迪布焦病毒埃博拉暴发为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(PHEIC)。暴发 已导致 11,000 多例病例和 5400 人死亡,成为刚果民主共和国历史上规模最大、死亡人数最多的埃博拉病毒 暴发,其中儿童受影响尤为严重。
规模、影响与风险
已记录超过 11,000 例病例和 5400 例死亡。由于准备和快速检测措施,刚果民主共和国的总体风险仍然很高,邻国也很高,而区域和全球层面的风险较低。
应对、状态与不确定性
当地响应者和全球合作伙伴正在取得进展。传播 在几个早期中心的进展正在放缓,而包括 Tumaini 试验和新的 疫苗 候选药物在内的临床试验正在迅速推进。世界卫生组织积极参与全球卫生架构改革和埃博拉应对工作。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Tedros: 'Six months ago, I declared the Bundibugyo virus Ebola outbreak in the DRC a Public Health Emergency of International Concern. Since then, over 11,000 cases and 5400 deaths have been recorded across eight provinces, making this the largest and - CGTN

News Brief
WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus declared the Bundibugyo virus Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on 17 May 2026. As of 15 November 2026, the outbreak has resulted in over 11,000 cases and 5400 deaths across eight provinces, making it the largest and deadliest Ebola outbreak in the DRC's history. Children have been disproportionately affected. Despite the challenges, local responders and global partners are making progress, with transmission slowing in several early epicentres and clinical trials advancing rapidly. The overall risk remains very high in the DRC and high in neighboring countries, but regional and global levels have lower risk due to preparedness and rapid detection measures. Tedros expressed gratitude for the efforts of Congolese healthcare workers and highlighted the importance of continued collaboration in the response.
Original source text (Korean)

Since then, over 11,000 cases and 5400 deaths have been recorded across eight provinces, making this the largest and deadliest Ebola outbreak in the country's history, with children bearing a heavy burden. 流动性数据预测本迪布焦病毒在刚果民主共和国的传播 — 新闻-医疗 - UA.NEWS

Mobility data predicted spread of Bundibugyo virus in DR Congo — News-Medical - UA.NEWS
新闻简报来自刚果民主共和国和国际合作伙伴的科学家分析了流动数据,以预测本迪布焦病毒病在伊图里和邻近省份的传播。这项发表在《科学》杂志上的研究发现,人类活动,而不是地理或人口规模,是病毒最初迅速传播的关键因素。从5月初到2026年6月1日,病例数大约每10天就会增加一倍。三周内,出现确诊病例的卫生区数量增加了12个,卫生区数量增加了49个。暴发于2026年5月15日宣布,研究凸显了流动性数据在预置医疗资源中的重要性。News-Medical 报道了这一发现,更多最新更新可在 UA.News Telegram 频道上获取。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年5月初至6月1日进行,暴发于2026年5月15日公布。新闻报道的发布日期为2026年11月17日。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在伊图里和刚果民主共和国 (DR Congo) 的邻近省份。该研究涉及来自 Mongbwalu、Bunia 和 Rwampara 的匿名手机数据。
事件
来自刚果民主共和国和国际合作伙伴的科学家分析了流动数据,以预测本迪布焦病毒病的传播。研究发现,人类流动,而不是地理或人口规模,是病毒最初迅速传播的关键因素。
规模、影响与风险
从5月初到2026年6月1日,病例数大约每10天就会增加一倍。三周多时间,确诊病例卫生区增加12个,卫生区数量增加49个。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究作者强调,移动性和出行时间数据可以帮助预先定位 监测、诊断和医疗护理资源。暴发 于 2026 年 5 月 15 日宣布,更多最新消息可在 UA.News Telegram 频道上查看。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mobility data predicted spread of Bundibugyo virus in DR Congo — News-Medical - UA.NEWS

News Brief
Scientists from the Democratic Republic of the Congo and international partners analyzed mobility data to predict the spread of Bundibugyo virus disease in Ituri and neighboring provinces. The study, published in the journal Science, found that human movement, rather than geography or population size, was the key factor in the rapid initial spread of the virus. From early May to June 1, 2026, the number of cases doubled approximately every 10 days. Over three weeks, the number of health zones with confirmed cases increased by 12, while the number of health areas increased by 49. The outbreak was declared on May 15, 2026, and the research highlights the importance of mobility data in pre-positioning medical resources. The findings were reported by News-Medical, and more current updates are available on the UA.News Telegram channel.
Original source text (English)
News Around the World Mobility data predicted spread of Bundibugyo virus in DR Congo — News-Medical UA.NEWS 17 November 2026 02:43 Scientists from the Democratic Republic of the Congo and international partners found that human movement, rather than geography or population size, was the key factor in the rapid initial spread of Bundibugyo virus disease in Ituri and neighboring provinces. This was reported by News-Medical , citing a study published in the journal Science. The first weeks of the outbreak Researchers combined detailed data on disease cases and testing with anonymized mobile phone data on population movements. According to their estimates, from early May to June 1, 2026, the number of cases doubled approximately every 10 days. The outbreak was declared on May 15, 2026. Over three weeks, the number of health zones with confirmed cases increased by 12, while the number of health areas increased by 49. More than 50 scientists from over 25 institutions took part in the work, including the DR Congo National Institute of Biomedical Research, the University of Kinshasa, Africa CDC, WHO, the University of Oxford, and the Pasteur Institute. More current news is available on the UA.News Telegram channel Telegram . Predicting the spread The team analyzed anonymized movement flows from Mongbwalu, Bunia, and Rwampara. A model based on these data predicted almost flawlessly which health zones would record cases of the disease and outperformed models relying solely on population size. The study authors noted that mobility and travel-time data available in the first days after an outbreak is reported can help pre-position surveillance, diagnostic, and medical care resources in areas to which the infection may spread next. Scale, impact and risk

世界新闻简讯:刚果民主共和国埃博拉病例翻倍,化学品暴露在出生前就开始,土耳其对欧盟-卢旺达转移计划的担忧 - 联合国新闻

World News in Brief: Ebola cases double in DR Congo, chemical exposure starts before birth, Türk concern over EU-Rwanda transfer plan - UN News
新闻简报根据世界卫生组织 (WHO) 的数据,截至 2026 年 11 月 17 日,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的埃博拉病毒 暴发 在三个月内增加了一倍多,报告病例数超过 11,000 例,死亡人数 5,400 人。儿童受到的影响尤其严重,占确诊病例的四分之一以上和死亡人数的三分之一,其中十分之七的五岁以下儿童确诊感染埃博拉病毒而死亡。世卫组织负责人警告说,暴发 传播到刚果民主共和国以外地区的风险并非理论上存在。尽管取得了一些进展,包括在大多数早期震中停止了快速 传播 以及增加了测试实验室,但 暴发 仍然不受控制。Bundibugyo 病毒的两个候选 疫苗 将被添加到正在进行的试验中。该报告还强调了出生前开始接触化学物质的更广泛影响,对健康可能产生长期影响。与此同时,联合国人权事务高级专员沃尔克·蒂尔克对将移民从欧盟国家转移到卢旺达的提议表示担忧,理由是存在人权风险以及需要采取全面的移民治理方法。
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至 2026 年 11 月 17 日 (N2),刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的埃博拉病毒 暴发 在三个月内增加了一倍多,目前已报告病例数超过 11,000 例,死亡人数超过 5,400 人。每周仍报告 400 至 500 例新病例 (N11)。
地点与对象
暴发 正在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 发生,特别是在八个省 (N2) 的 67 个卫生区。儿童被视为弱势群体,超过四分之一的确诊病例和三分之一的死亡涉及儿童(N4)。
事件
世界卫生组织总干事谭德塞报告称,刚果民主共和国的埃博拉病毒暴发在三个月内增加了一倍多,确诊病例超过11,000例,死亡人数超过5,400人(N2)。他还指出,三分之二的确诊死亡病例发生在埃博拉 治疗 中心之外,这表明感染识别较晚 (N11)。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 已在刚果民主共和国 (N2) 造成 11,000 多例病例和 5,400 人死亡。在确诊埃博拉病毒的五岁以下儿童中,十分之七死亡 (N4)。世卫组织负责人强调,刚果民主共和国境外的风险并非理论上存在(N5)。
应对、状态与不确定性
在阻止大多数早期震中的快速 传播 以及将能够检测病毒的实验室数量从两个增加到两打以上 (N8) 方面已经取得了进展。Bundibugyo 病毒的两个候选 疫苗 将被添加到正在进行的试验 (N9) 中。但暴发并未完全受控,风险仍然较大(N10)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

World News in Brief: Ebola cases double in DR Congo, chemical exposure starts before birth, Türk concern over EU-Rwanda transfer plan - UN News

News Brief
The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) has more than doubled in three months, with over 11,000 cases and 5,400 deaths reported as of 17 November 2026, according to the World Health Organization (WHO). Children are particularly affected, accounting for more than a quarter of confirmed cases and a third of deaths, with seven in ten children under five with confirmed Ebola dying. The WHO head warned that the risk of the outbreak spreading beyond the DRC is not theoretical. Despite progress, including the halting of rapid transmission in most early epicentres and an increase in testing laboratories, the outbreak remains uncontrolled. Two candidate vaccines for the Bundibugyo virus will be added to an ongoing trial. The report also highlights the broader implications of chemical exposure starting before birth, with potential long-term effects on health. Meanwhile, UN human rights chief Volker Türk expressed concerns over a proposal to transfer migrants from EU countries to Rwanda, citing human rights risks and the need for a comprehensive approach to migration governance.
Original source text (English)

Facebook Twitter Print Email World News in Brief: Ebola cases double in DR Congo, chemical exposure starts before birth, Türk concern over EU-Rwanda transfer plan 17 November 2026 Health The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) has more than doubled in three months, with more than 11,000 cases and 5,400 deaths now reported in 67 health zones across eight provinces, the head of the World Health Organization (WHO) said on Tuesday. Tweet URL Opening the third meeting of WHO’s Emergency Committee on the Bundibugyo virus disease epidemic, Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus said children were “paying a terrible price”. They account for more than a quarter of confirmed cases and a third of deaths, and seven in ten children under five with confirmed Ebola have died. “The risk beyond the DRC’s borders is not theoretical,” he said. Kenya has reported nine cases linked to an imported case, including two deaths, and Nigeria has reported one imported case. He also expressed concern that some countries continue to apply blanket travel restrictions, which hinder the response and discourage countries from reporting outbreaks quickly. Progress made Elaborating on the progress made, Dr Tedros said rapid transmission has been halted in most of the early epicentres, and the number of laboratories able to test for the virus has risen from two to more than two dozen. Two candidate vaccines developed specifically for Bundibugyo virus will be added to an ongoing vaccine trial in the coming weeks. “But progress in some places is not control of the outbreak as a whole,” Dr. Tedros warned. Between 400 and 500 new cases are still reported every week, and two-thirds of confirmed deaths occur outside Ebola treatment centres, a sign that infections are being identified too late. Exposure to hormone-disrupting chemicals can begin before birth Exposure to endocrine-disrupting chemicals (EDCs) can begin before birth, with a broader range of effects on human and environmental health than previously understood, a new report released by the UN Enviornment Programme (UNEP) and WHO revealed on Tuesday. The chemicals, which can interfere with the body’s hormones, have been detected in blood, breast milk and cord blood. Early exposure can have lasting effects on brain development, growth and reproductive health. Tweet URL The State of the Science on Endocrine-Disrupting Chemicals 2026 is the first major global update since 2012. It finds stronger evidence linking EDCs to effects on fertility, brain development, thyroid and other hormone regulation, metabolism, immunity and cardiovascular health. In fish, birds, amphibians and mammals, exposure has been linked to impaired reproduction, development and behaviour. ‘Long-lasting’ impact “These effects can be long-lasting and, in some cases, irreversible,” said Sheila Aggarwal Khan, Director of UNEP’s Industry and Economy Division. More than 350,000 chemicals and chemical mixtures are registered for production and use globally, yet only a small fraction have been evaluated for endocrine-disrupting properties. According to the study, the global cost of diseases associated with EDC exposure could amount to one per cent or more of global GDP. “Protecting health means preventing harmful exposures before they occur,” said Bruce Gordon, acting Director of WHO’s Department of Environment, Climate Change, One Health and Migration. The report was launched at the first International Conference of the Global Framework on Chemicals, which runs in Geneva until 20 November. Türk raises rights concerns over EU-Rwanda transfer proposal UN human rights chief Volker Türk has raised concern about the human rights risks of a proposal to transfer people from five European Union countries to Rwanda, “where they have no genuine connection and which has its own set of human rights challenges”. Mr. Türk said his Office was not privy to the text of the proposal. “The solution does not lie in single measures – often very costly – of finding countries far away to deal with one aspect of migration governance, or in externalising human rights obligations,” he said. Instead, he called for the drivers of migration to be tackled head-on, including through investment that creates viable opportunities at home. Those drivers include human rights challenges, huge inequalities, an inequitable economic and financial system that disadvantages many countries in the Global South, a surge in violent conflicts and heightened geopolitical tensions. At the same time, societies in the Global North are rapidly ageing and grappling with labour shortages, Mr. Türk noted. He called for a comprehensive approach to migration governance anchored in human rights, including expanded regular migration pathways. health Migrants and refugees Facebook Twitter Print Email Back to top

The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) has more than doubled in three months, with more than 11,000 cases and 5,400 deaths now reported as of 17 November 2026 (N2). Between 400 and 500 new cases are still reported every week (N11).

暴发 重建表明,人类旅行导致埃博拉病毒在刚果民主共和国传播 - CIDRAP

Human travel drove Ebola spread in DR Congo, outbreak reconstruction suggests - CIDRAP
新闻简报CIDRAP 的一份新报告表明,人类旅行在刚果民主共和国(刚果民主共和国)埃博拉病毒的传播中发挥了关键作用。该研究表明,该病毒很可能在 2026 年 5 月中旬正式宣布疫情爆发前几周就已经传播。该报告强调了了解 传播 动态的重要性,特别是在旅行模式的背景下。它还指出,儿童占 暴发 地区埃博拉死亡人数的 35%,这凸显了某些人群的脆弱性。虽然该研究提供了对 暴发 传播的见解,但它没有详细说明具体的应对措施或干预措施。该报告对 暴发 的进展以及人类运动对疾病 传播 的潜在影响进行了批判性分析。研究结果基于 暴发 重建,不包括确诊病例或详细的公共卫生措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
研究表明,刚果民主共和国的埃博拉暴发很可能在2026年5月中旬宣布之前几周就开始传播。报告发布日期为2026年11月17日。
地点与对象
该报告重点关注埃博拉病毒 暴发 发生地刚果民主共和国(DR Congo)。它提到儿童占 暴发 埃博拉死亡人数的 35%。
事件
一项研究表明,人类旅行在刚果民主共和国埃博拉病毒的传播中发挥了重要作用。该报告基于 暴发 重建,并归因于 CIDRAP。
规模、影响与风险
据报道,刚果民主共和国的 暴发 病例已接近 11,000 例。儿童占死亡人数的 35%,该研究强调了人类旅行在病毒传播中的作用。由于存在进一步 传播 的可能性,该风险被描述为重大。
应对、状态与不确定性
该报告基于 暴发 重建,表明埃博拉病毒的传播是由人类旅行驱动的。它没有提供具体反应或调查的细节,但指出美国已于 2000 年实现麻疹消除状态,这是一个单独的问题。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Human travel drove Ebola spread in DR Congo, outbreak reconstruction suggests - CIDRAP

News Brief
A new report from CIDRAP suggests that human travel played a key role in the spread of Ebola in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DR Congo). The study indicates that the virus was likely circulating weeks before the epidemic was officially declared in mid-May 2026. The report highlights the importance of understanding transmission dynamics, particularly in the context of travel patterns. It also notes that children make up 35% of Ebola deaths in the outbreak, underscoring the vulnerability of certain populations. While the study provides insights into the outbreak's spread, it does not detail specific responses or interventions. The report serves as a critical analysis of the outbreak's progression and the potential impact of human movement on disease transmission. The findings are based on outbreak reconstruction and do not include confirmed cases or detailed public health measures.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果埃博拉 暴发 六个月后冲突、饥饿加剧危机,无法控制 - 日本时报

Congo Ebola outbreak defies control as conflict, hunger fuel crisis six months in - The Japan Times
新闻简报刚果民主共和国历史上最大规模的埃博拉病毒 暴发 已经发生六个月,尽管采取了遏制措施,但该病毒仍在继续传播。截至2026年11月16日,已有超过11,000人被感染,据报道有5,500人死亡,死亡率接近49%。暴发 已覆盖 8 个省的 68 个卫生区,许多死亡发生在社区而不是 治疗 中心。卫生机构缺乏基本的基础设施,冲突、饥饿和不信任使应对工作变得更加复杂。国际救援委员会指出,对埃博拉治疗中心的恐惧导致了谣言和对隔离的抵制。虽然默克公司的 Ervebo 疫苗 正在使用,但它仅被批准用于扎伊尔埃博拉病毒,并且没有经过验证的 治疗 用于驱动当前 暴发 的 Bundibugyo 菌株。暴力事件进一步加剧了局势,在某些地区,武装团体造成的死亡人数比病毒本身还要多。改善接触者追踪和让私人医疗服务提供者参与的努力仍在继续,但 暴发 仍然是公共卫生官员面临的重大挑战。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 六个月后,活动日期为 2026 年 5 月 15 日,发布日期为 2026 年 11 月 16 日。
地点与对象
Democratic Republic of Congo, particularly in North Kivu and South Kivu provinces, with 14 health zones affected across eight provinces. The population at risk includes 29 million people facing acute food insecurity, with 11 million in the eastern provinces.
事件
由于冲突、饥饿、不信任和卫生系统紧张等因素,刚果民主共和国的埃博拉病毒 暴发 已无法得到控制。暴发 已持续六个月,截至 2026 年 11 月 16 日,报告了超过 11,000 例感染和 5,500 例死亡。
规模、影响与风险
自5月15日治疗开始以来,已有超过11,000人被感染,5,500人死亡。死亡率接近49%,约三分之二的死亡发生在社区而不是治疗中心。暴发 已覆盖 8 个省的 68 个卫生区,而缺乏针对 Bundibugyo 菌株的经过验证的 疫苗 或 治疗 增加了风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
由于冲突和不安全局势,卫生官员正在努力追踪接触者和隔离工作。暴发 正在通过使用默克公司的 Ervebo 疫苗 得到解决,该药物被批准用于扎伊尔埃博拉病毒,并且针对 Bundibugyo 特异性 疫苗 的临床试验正在进行中。恐惧、不信任以及在冲突地区提供护理的挑战也阻碍了应对工作。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Congo Ebola outbreak defies control as conflict, hunger fuel crisis six months in - The Japan Times

News Brief
Six months into the largest Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo's history, the virus continues to spread despite containment efforts. As of November 16, 2026, over 11,000 people have been infected, with 5,500 deaths reported, resulting in a nearly 49% fatality rate. The outbreak has reached 68 health zones across eight provinces, with many deaths occurring in communities rather than treatment centers. Health facilities lack basic infrastructure, and conflict, hunger, and mistrust are complicating the response. The International Rescue Committee noted that fear of Ebola treatment centers has led to rumors and resistance to isolation. While Merck & Co.’s Ervebo vaccine is being used, it is approved only for Zaire ebolavirus, and there is no proven treatment for the Bundibugyo strain driving the current outbreak. The situation is further exacerbated by violence, with armed groups causing more deaths than the virus itself in some areas. Efforts to improve contact tracing and engage private health providers are ongoing, but the outbreak remains a significant challenge for public health officials.
Original source text (Japanese)
Subscribe Digital Print November 18, 2026 Subscribe Digital Print Cyberattacks APEC Foreign community in Japan Latest News Today's print edition Home Delivery JAPAN Politics Society Crime & Legal Science & Health Explainer History WORLD Politics Crime & Legal Science & Health Society ASIA PACIFIC Politics Crime & Legal Science & Health Society BUSINESS Companies Economy Markets Tech SPORTS Sumo Soccer Baseball Basketball Tennis Olympics More sports OPINION Editorials Commentary Geoeconomic Briefing Environment CLIMATE CHANGE Energy SUSTAINABILITY WILDLIFE EARTH SCIENCE LIFE Travel Digital Food & Drink Style & Design Language Lifestyle CULTURE Film Books Music Art TV & Streaming Stage Entertainment news COMMUNITY Voices Issues How-tos Our Lives My Account My Bookmarks Logout Subscribe for more access WORLD / Science & Health Ebola outbreak defies control as war and hunger fuel crisis six months in People pass through at an Ebola health checkpoint on the outskirts of Biakato, Congo, a small town deep in the rainforest which now accounts for as many as one-quarter of the sprawling country’s new Ebola cases, on Oct. 19. | Daniel Berehulak / The New York Times BLOOMBERG SHARE/SAVE X Facebook LinkedIn Reddit Bluesky Threads Email Print Bookmark story Copy link Nov 16, 2026 Listen to this article 7 min Six months into the biggest Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo’s history, containing the virus increasingly depends on solving problems far beyond Ebola — hunger, armed conflict, mistrust and a health system already buckling under the strain. More than 11,000 people have been infected and 5,500 have died since the outbreak was declared May 15. For months, Ebola has been killing roughly 240 people a week, the equivalent of about one and a half Boeing Co. 737 passenger jets crashing every week. “It’s a systems problem compounded by basically geography, insecurity, conflict, poor roads, mistrust,” said William Clemmer, a physician who has worked in Congo for three decades and is senior regional health adviser for Corus International based in Goma. Clemmer, 69, who has worked on four Ebola outbreaks, said many health facilities entered the current crisis without reliable water, electricity, sterilization, waste disposal or supply systems. Delivering protective equipment can only accomplish so much if clinics lack the basic infrastructure needed to use it safely, he said. Congo’s own data illustrate the problem. As of Nov. 14, the country had recorded 11,376 confirmed infections and 5,568 deaths, for a fatality rate of almost 49%. The outbreak had reached 68 health zones across eight provinces. In North Kivu province, 153 Ebola patients and suspected cases were being cared for in ordinary health facilities outside designated treatment and isolation centers on Nov. 14. People who aren’t identified and isolated can continue spreading Ebola at home, in clinics and during funerals. A local shop keeper is helped at a makeshift treatment center for Ebola patients in Biakato, Congo, on Oct. 16. | Daniel Berehulak / The New York Times About two-thirds of Ebola deaths have recently occurred in communities rather than treatment centers, according to Medecins Sans Frontieres. “When two-thirds of our deaths occur in local communities, that basically tells us that either people are being detected too late or they’re not reaching care at all,” Clemmer said. “That is an indication not just of failure of surveillance, but also of access, trust and security.” Fear factor The International Rescue Committee said its teams recently documented 84 rumors, including a recurring belief that people taken to Ebola treatment centers “do not return home alive.” The group said fear is discouraging some women from seeking routine medical care, including postnatal services. The outbreak’s roughly 50% fatality rate makes that reluctance understandable, Clemmer said. Unlike the 2018-2020 Ebola epidemic in eastern Congo, responders don’t yet have a vaccine or treatment proven to work against the Bundibugyo strain driving the current outbreak. Telling families that a relative needs to enter a treatment center where many patients are dying can fuel resistance, he said. The consequences extend far beyond Ebola. Almost 29 million people in Congo already face acute food insecurity, a number projected to exceed 30 million in the first half of next year, according to United Nations agencies. More than 11 million of them are in Ituri, North Kivu and South Kivu, the eastern provinces at the center of the country’s humanitarian crisis. Conflict displaces families from their farms and livelihoods, while Ebola restrictions and fear can further disrupt farming and trade. For health officials, that creates another obstacle: asking someone exposed to Ebola to isolate is harder when leaving home may be necessary to earn money or find food. “These people have the responsibility of working, of getting food for their family,” Clemmer said. Violence poses a more direct threat, with armed groups killing more people than Ebola itself in some affected areas, Julien Harneis, the U.N.’s senior Ebola response coordinator, said last week. Health care workers carry the corpse of a victim from a makeshift Ebola treatment center in Biakato, Congo on Oct. 18. | Daniel Berehulak / The New York Times Over one recent weekend, more people were murdered by the Allied Democratic Forces (ADF) than died from the virus, he said in a post marking six months since the outbreak was declared. The violence is also making the epidemic harder to stop. Harneis said one of the response’s most pressing challenges is finding ways to improve contact tracing in conflict areas where attacks by the ADF and other armed groups are impeding the work. He also called for greater engagement with private health providers, where he said most people initially seek care. Scientific hope There are reasons for optimism. Transmission has declined in some former hotspots, neighboring countries have so far avoided sustained spread, and clinical trials under way in Congo could eventually provide vaccines and treatments specifically suited to Bundibugyo Ebola. Thousands of people are already receiving Merck & Co.’s Ervebo vaccine while scientists investigate whether the shot, approved against Zaire ebolavirus, can also protect against Bundibugyo. Africa CDC said emerging evidence suggests it may provide some cross-protection and supports its use in high-risk children while the evaluation continues. Meanwhile, two vaccines designed specifically against Bundibugyo — one developed by the University of Oxford with the Serum Institute of India and another from Moderna — are set to enter a late-stage trial in Congo. Researchers will test whether they can protect people exposed to the virus from developing severe disease or dying, with results expected in early 2027. The latest government report underscores both the urgency and the scale of the effort. More than 3,300 people have been vaccinated in North Kivu, leaving just 247 doses there and prompting an urgent request for 10,000 more. Researchers are also testing experimental treatments and post-exposure therapies as part of several trials. Those scientific advances may create another challenge. Wellcome, a London-based health research foundation, warned that financing and procurement arrangements need to be established before trial results arrive so that successful vaccines or medicines can quickly be manufactured and delivered at scale. “The measure of success of this outbreak response will not be whether new tools are developed, but whether those at risk can benefit from them,” said Charlotte Watts, Wellcome’s director of solutions. For now, however, the epidemic is still being fought largely with the basic tools of outbreak control: finding infected people, tracing their contacts, isolating patients, protecting health workers and persuading frightened communities to cooperate. Those measures can work. The difficulty is making them work simultaneously across a vast area affected by armed conflict and a health system that was struggling long before Ebola arrived. “This is an outbreak that with good public health measures can be controlled,” Clemmer said. “It’s just we need those measures” to overcome the systems problems standing in their way. View five news elements
Time
Six months into the outbreak, with the event date being May 15, 2026, and the publication date on November 16, 2026.
Location and population
Democratic Republic of Congo, particularly in North Kivu and South Kivu provinces, with 14 health zones affected across eight provinces. The population at risk includes 29 million people facing acute food insecurity, with 11 million in the eastern provinces.
Event
An Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo has defied control due to factors like conflict, hunger, mistrust, and a strained health system. The outbreak has been ongoing for six months, with over 11,000 infections and 5,500 deaths reported as of November 16, 2026.
Scale, impact and risk
More than 11,000 people have been infected and 5,500 have died since the outbreak began on May 15. The fatality rate is nearly 49%, and about two-thirds of deaths occur in communities rather than treatment centers. The outbreak has reached 68 health zones across eight provinces, and the lack of a proven vaccine or treatment for the Bundibugyo strain adds to the risk.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials are struggling with contact tracing and isolation efforts due to conflict and insecurity. The outbreak is being addressed through the use of Merck & Co.’s Ervebo vaccine, which is approved for Zaire ebolavirus, and there are ongoing clinical trials for Bundibugyo-specific vaccines. The response is also hindered by fear, mistrust, and the challenges of delivering care in conflict zones.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Statement on US Travel Restrictions Related to the Bundibugyo Ebola Outbreak - Home – Africa CDC

News Brief
Africa CDC issued a statement on November 15, 2026, regarding US travel restrictions related to the Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak. The statement highlights the organization's role in supporting the Democratic Republic of the Congo government to enhance transparency and accountability in Ebola response funding. It also outlines broader initiatives such as the Africa’s Health Security and Sovereignty (AHSS) agenda, which aims to empower African nations to finance and govern their health systems. However, the evidence does not provide specific details on the outbreak's scale, impact, or risk level. The statement is part of a larger context of public health efforts and does not include confirmed cases, deaths, or exposures. The response status is under investigation, with no further details on the outbreak's current status or containment measures.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果应对埃博拉病毒 暴发 6 个月后仍处于失控状态 - PBS

Ebola outbreak remains out of control in Congo 6 months into response - PBS
新闻简报An Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of Congo remains out of control six months into the response. Over 11,000 cases have been recorded, with nearly half of the infected individuals dying, according to government data. This outbreak is the deadliest in Congo's history, surpassing the death toll from the West African outbreak between 2014 and 2016. The Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention report that 80% of new cases are detected outside official health surveillance, indicating widespread undetected circulation. Community transmission is high, with 35% of deaths among children and nearly 72% of infected children under 5 succumbing to the disease. The virus has spread to eight provinces, and fears of continent-wide transmission persist. Basic containment measures, including contact tracing and isolation, are inadequate, and health workers have gone on strike due to unpaid wages. Over $700 million in aid remains unaccounted for, and conflict in the region has further complicated response efforts.
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 于 2026 年 5 月中旬宣布后六个月,截至 2026 年 11 月 15 日正在进行的报告。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国,特别是东部省份伊图里省和北基伍省,影响了社区和医疗设施。
事件
根据政府数据,由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发 仍处于失控状态,已记录超过 11,000 例病例,其中近一半感染者已死亡。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 是刚果历史上最致命的一次,80% 的新病例是在官方卫生部门 监测 之外发现的。社区传播高,35%的死亡是儿童,其中近72%的5岁以下感染儿童死亡。该病毒已传播到八个省份,对整个非洲大陆 传播 的担忧持续存在。
应对、状态与不确定性
Containment efforts are inadequate, with basic measures like contact tracing and isolation not sufficient. Health workers have gone on strike due to unpaid wages, and over $700 million in aid remains unaccounted for. Conflict and attacks on health workers and facilities further hinder response efforts.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ebola outbreak remains out of control in Congo 6 months into response - PBS

News Brief
An Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of Congo remains out of control six months into the response. Over 11,000 cases have been recorded, with nearly half of the infected individuals dying, according to government data. This outbreak is the deadliest in Congo's history, surpassing the death toll from the West African outbreak between 2014 and 2016. The Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention report that 80% of new cases are detected outside official health surveillance, indicating widespread undetected circulation. Community transmission is high, with 35% of deaths among children and nearly 72% of infected children under 5 succumbing to the disease. The virus has spread to eight provinces, and fears of continent-wide transmission persist. Basic containment measures, including contact tracing and isolation, are inadequate, and health workers have gone on strike due to unpaid wages. Over $700 million in aid remains unaccounted for, and conflict in the region has further complicated response efforts.
Original source text (English)
FILE PHOTO: Congolese medical workers wearing personal protective equipment carry the wooden coffin containing the body of Dr Marie-France Shogogo, who died at an Ebola treatment centre after contracting the virus, as aid agencies intensify efforts to contain a new Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus, during her burial under specialized safe and dignified burial protocols at Nyamurongo Cemetery in Bunia town, Ituri province, Democratic Republic of Congo, on Oct. 31, 2026. Photo by Gradel Muyisa Mumbere/Reuters BUNIA, Congo (AP) — Six months after an Ebola outbreak was declared in Congo, the disease remains out of control and efforts to contain it are faltering. More than 11,000 cases have been recorded, and nearly half of those infected have died, according to government data. It is the deadliest of Congo's 17 outbreaks and is on track to surpass the death toll from the West African outbreak that killed 11,000 people between 2014 and 2016. READ MORE: Congo's Ebola deaths top 5,000 as fear of continent-wide spread mounts Basic containment measures, including contact tracing and isolation, remain inadequate, raising doubts about authorities' ability to halt the outbreak. Experts say there is little chance of bringing it under control within the next year. By the time the outbreak was announced in mid-May, 43 deaths had already been recorded, suggesting the virus had circulated undetected for an extended period. It has since spread to eight of the country's 26 provinces, moving at a pace aid groups have likened to wildfire. New hotspots continue to emerge, stretching an already strained health care system. Questions over funding and management Community transmission remains high. According to the Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, 80% of new cases are being detected outside health surveillance, meaning most infections have not been tracked by authorities. One-third of deaths occur at home or elsewhere in the community, where burials and other procedures are often conducted without specialist supervision, increasing the risk of transmission. READ MORE: Congo's confirmed Ebola cases surpass 9,000 as containment efforts falter Health workers have repeatedly gone on strike over pay that is months overdue, sometimes leaving patients with confirmed infections without treatment. Aid groups have also questioned how the response is being managed. Last month, the Africa CDC raised concerns about more than $700 million in Ebola aid that remains unaccounted for. "A troubling disconnect between the considerable resources mobilised for the response and what is actually reaching patients, frontline workers, and communities continues," said the humanitarian organization Doctors Without Borders, known by its French acronym MSF. Children face high death rates Children are particularly vulnerable to Ebola, especially those with preexisting conditions such as malnutrition, experts say. Most children affected by the disease have not received specialized care, contributing to high death rates. According to UNICEF, children accounted for 35% of deaths recorded through Nov. 4. Children under 5 accounted for 21% of all deaths, and nearly 72% of infected children in that age group have died. Conflict and attacks hamper response Health workers face difficult conditions as Ebola prevention and containment measures clash with social and cultural practices. Since the outbreak began, health workers have been attacked, some fatally, adding to the dangers of working in an already volatile region. Health facilities have also come under attack. The outbreak has been concentrated in eastern Congo, particularly Ituri and North Kivu, where communities face both the epidemic and armed conflict. READ MORE: Inside the study for a remedy to fight Congo's deadliest Ebola outbreak Fighting between government forces and hundreds of rebel groups, including the Rwanda-backed M23, had already helped create one of the world's most severe humanitarian crises. The Allied Democratic Forces, an Islamic State-affiliated rebel group, also attacks civilians. The virus's rapid spread has raised fears of wider transmission across Africa. Nigeria, Kenya and Uganda have confirmed cases, making this the most geographically widespread Ebola outbreak on record. The Associated Press receives financial support for global health and development coverage in Africa from the Gates Foundation. The AP is solely responsible for all content. Find AP's standards for working with philanthropies, a list of supporters and funded coverage areas at AP.org. Photo by Gradel Muyisa Mumbere/Reuters

Democratic Republic of Congo, particularly in the eastern provinces of Ituri and North Kivu, affecting communities and healthcare facilities.
Event
An Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus remains out of control, with over 11,000 cases recorded and nearly half of those infected having died, according to government data.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak is the deadliest in Congo's history, with 80% of new cases detected outside official health surveillance. Community transmission is high, and 35% of deaths are among children, with nearly 72% of infected children under 5 dying. The virus has spread to eight provinces, and fears of continent-wide transmission persist.
Response, status and uncertainty
Containment efforts are inadequate, with basic measures like contact tracing and isolation not sufficient. Health workers have gone on strike due to unpaid wages, and over $700 million in aid remains unaccounted for. Conflict and attacks on health workers and facilities further hinder response efforts.
原文 / Source
Published: 2026-11-15  |  Publisher: Anadolu Ajansı

3,000 recover from Ebola in DR Congo: WHO - Anadolu Ajansı
新闻简报世界卫生组织 (WHO) 报告称,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 已有约 3,000 人从 埃博拉病毒病 中康复,这标志着该国抗击有史以来最大规模 暴发 的斗争取得了进展。自 2026 年 5 月以来,由本迪布焦病毒引起的 暴发 已导致超过 11,000 例确诊病例和 5,400 例死亡。传播 在几个早期震中的进展正在放缓,而包括 Tumaini 试验和新的 疫苗 候选药物在内的临床试验正在迅速推进。世卫组织已宣布 暴发 为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件,并敦促安全人道主义援助准入、停止敌对行动并增加资金以支持应对工作。该组织强调,消灭埃博拉病毒首先要从社区开始,呼吁通过富有同情心的护理建立信任,并敦促捐助者缩小关键的资金缺口。
查看新闻五要素
时间
自 2026 年 5 月至 11 月 15 日起。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国,8 个省设有 67 个卫生区。
事件
世卫组织报告称,刚果民主共和国约有 3000 人从 埃博拉病毒病 中康复,该国正面临有记录以来最大规模的 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
已记录超过 11,000 例确诊病例,其中 5,400 例死亡。传播 在几个早期震中的进展正在放缓,而临床试验正在迅速推进。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

3,000 recover from Ebola in DR Congo: WHO - Anadolu Ajansı

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) reported that approximately 3,000 people have recovered from Ebola virus disease in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC), marking progress in the country's fight against its largest outbreak on record. The outbreak, caused by the Bundibugyo virus, has resulted in over 11,000 confirmed cases and 5,400 deaths since May 2026. Transmission is slowing in several early epicentres, and clinical trials, including the Tumaini trial and new vaccine candidates, are advancing rapidly. WHO has declared the outbreak a public health emergency of international concern and urged for safe humanitarian access, cessation of hostilities, and increased funding to support the response. The organization emphasized that ending Ebola starts with communities, calling for trust-building through compassionate care and urging donors to close critical funding gaps.
Original source text (English)
Health World Africa 3,000 recover from Ebola in DR Congo: WHO Since May, over 11,000 confirmed cases, including 5,400 deaths, have been recorded, according to official data James Tasamba 15 November 2026 • Update : 15 November 2026 KIGALI, Rwanda About 3,000 people have recovered from Ebola virus disease in the Democratic Republic of Congo and returned home, thanks to the efforts of health workers, the World Health Organization (WHO) said Sunday, as the country battles its largest outbreak on record. World Health Organization Director-General Tedros Ghebreyesus expressed deep gratitude to the nurses, doctors, community health workers, contact tracers and burial teams who he said are working under extraordinary pressure to save lives. "Amid immense hardship, local responders and global partners, under the leadership of the government, are driving hard-won progress. Transmission is slowing in several early epicentres, and clinical trials, including the Tumaini trial and new vaccine candidates, are advancing rapidly," Tedros said in a post on the US social media platform X. WHO declared the Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus in Congo a public health emergency of international concern six months ago. Since then, more than 11,000 confirmed cases, including 5,400 deaths, have been recorded in 67 health zones across eight provinces. Tedros called for safe humanitarian access and a cessation of hostilities so health workers can reach every affected area without fear. Noting that ending Ebola starts and ends with communities, Tedros urged Ebola responders to build trust through listening, respect and compassionate care so people seek treatment early, when lives can be saved. He urged donors to step up and close critical funding gaps to sustain the response, saying WHO would remain steadfast in its support for the people of Congo, working with the government and partners until zero cases remain. Earlier this month, Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) Director-General Jean Kaseya said two-thirds of the $2.9 billion in funding mobilized for the Ebola response on the continent had yet to be disbursed. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果的埃博拉病毒 暴发 的传播速度比以往任何时候都快,并且以创纪录的速度导致死亡 - 现代外交

Congo’s Ebola Outbreak Is Spreading Faster Than Ever — and Killing at Record Pace - Modern Diplomacy
新闻简报The Ebola outbreak in eastern Democratic Republic of Congo, caused by the Bundibugyo strain, has remained uncontrolled for six months since its declaration on May 15, 2026. As of November 16, 2026, over 11,000 cases and more than 5,500 deaths have been reported, with the rate of infections and fatalities doubling compared to the first six months of the West Africa outbreak in 2014-16. This is Congo’s 17th Ebola outbreak, surpassing the previous largest from 2018-2020. Challenges include security issues, poor coordination, and community skepticism, with health workers facing attacks and strikes over unpaid salaries. Despite $2.9 billion pledged for managing the outbreak, less than half has been released. The World Health Organization has called on donors to collaborate with the Congolese government to ensure effective resource utilization. The situation remains critical, with the outbreak spreading faster than ever and killing at a record pace.
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 自 2026 年 5 月 15 日以来一直在进行,截至 2026 年 11 月 16 日报告的病例数和死亡人数不断增加。该文章于 2026 年 11 月 17 日发表。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在刚果民主共和国东部,特别是北基伍省,涉及 Bundibugyo 毒株。该地区的卫生工作者和社区受到影响。
事件
刚果民主共和国东部的埃博拉病毒 暴发 在宣布六个月后仍未得到控制,病例数和死亡人数不断增加。Bundibugyo 菌株是 暴发 的罪魁祸首,它的传播速度比以前的任何 暴发 都要快。
规模、影响与风险
自 2026 年 5 月 15 日以来,已报告超过 11,000 人感染,超过 5,500 人死亡。与 2014-16 年西非 暴发 的前六个月相比,感染率和死亡率翻了一番。这是刚果第17起埃博拉疫情暴发,超过了此前2018-2020年最大的暴发。
应对、状态与不确定性
Security issues and poor coordination are major challenges, with health workers facing attacks and communities skeptical of outsiders. There are reports of health workers striking over unpaid salaries, slowing the response. $2.9 billion has been pledged for managing the outbreak, but less than half has been released. The World Health Organization urges donors to collaborate with the Congolese government to ensure effective use of resources.

埃博拉药物的获取规划在临床试验期间取得进展 - Unitaid

Access planning for Ebola medicines moves ahead during clinical trials - Unitaid
新闻简报在正在进行的临床试验中,国际药品采购机制及其合作伙伴正在推进用于治疗埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 的候选药物的获取计划,包括奥贝昔韦和瑞德西韦。该计划涉及确保不同地区制造商的供应承诺,以确保试验有效后可在 127 个国家/地区供货。吉利德宣布与 Dr. Reddy’s Laboratories、Eva Pharma 和 Ferozsons Laboratories 签署免版税自愿许可协议,如果获得批准,将允许生产和分销奥贝昔韦。此外,吉利德正在扩大其现有的瑞德西韦(一种用于 COVID-19 的 抗病毒药物)许可,以涵盖 BVD。这些努力是为治疗和诊断的潜在供应和采用做好准备的更广泛战略的一部分,确保各国能够在临床证据支持其使用的情况下立即引入这些工具。截至 2026 年 11 月 17 日报告的进展情况是基于国际药品采购机制、世卫组织和其他利益攸关方在埃博拉应对工作中的合作。

全球性,重点关注许可证覆盖的 127 个国家/地区。
事件
在正在进行的临床试验中,国际药品采购机制及其合作伙伴正在推进候选埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 药物(包括奥贝昔韦和瑞德西韦)的获取计划。该事件已根据国际药品采购机制 (Unitaid) 和吉利德 (Gilead) 的公告得到确认。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Access planning for Ebola medicines moves ahead during clinical trials - Unitaid

News Brief
Unitaid and its partners are advancing access planning for candidate medicines to treat Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD), including obeldesivir and remdesivir, during ongoing clinical trials. The initiative involves securing supply commitments from manufacturers across different regions to ensure availability in 127 countries if trials prove effective. Gilead has announced royalty-free voluntary licensing agreements with Dr. Reddy’s Laboratories, Eva Pharma, and Ferozsons Laboratories, which will allow production and distribution of obeldesivir if approved. Additionally, Gilead is expanding its existing licenses for remdesivir, an antiviral used for COVID-19, to cover BVD. These efforts are part of broader strategies to prepare for potential supply and adoption of therapeutics and diagnostics, ensuring countries can introduce these tools without delay if clinical evidence supports their use. The progress is reported as of November 17, 2026, and is based on the collaboration between Unitaid, WHO, and other stakeholders in the Ebola response.
Original source text (English)
